This file is the mailbox between the two AIs working on ch. 19. Read it at the start of every session and after every push.

**Owner, 2026-10-24:** Claude Code is out of this loop. Do not wait on `STATUS: REVIEW`. After the report is pushed, take the next step on the route list. `STATUS: OWNER` still stops the work.

## Protocol

- **`STATUS: BENCH`**: Grok Build's turn. Do the task under "Current task", within the standing rules below. **Start it the moment you see `STATUS: BENCH` with a task you have not reported on yet: do not wait for the owner or for a "go" (owner, 2026-10-27). A review that names a next step is the instruction to do that step now. Only `STATUS: REVIEW` or `STATUS: OWNER`, or an unanswered STUCK, means wait.** When you finish a step and the current task already names the following step, continue into it and report once at the end, unless the task says to report first.
  - Write results as before: `radio_firmware/results.md`, plus the ch. 19 §10 progress log.
  - Then edit this file: set `STATUS: REVIEW`, and replace "Last report" with 2–5 lines (what was done, the key numbers, the end state).
  - Commit and push. Always `git pull --rebase` before pushing.
- **`STATUS: REVIEW`**: Claude Code's turn. It reviews the push and writes the review in `radio_firmware/reviews.md`, puts the next task under "Current task", sets `STATUS: BENCH` and pushes.
- **`STATUS: STUCK`** (owner, 2026-10-25 19:45): **Grok's way out of any obstacle.** Use it the moment one of these is true: a step's instruction is unclear or two instructions conflict; a measurement contradicts the pass list and the next move is not written; the module, the phone, the HackRF or SignalDeck is not behaving and the recovery below has not brought it back within one try; or 45 minutes have gone into one obstacle without a result. Then: (1) write a **Stuck** block right under "## Last report": what you were doing (the step and sub-step), the last three things tried with their exact outcome (commands, replies, numbers, timestamps), the current state of the module (tag, channel, frequency, power, keyed or not), the phone (SignalDeck running, stock app paused, battery and zone) and the HackRF (running, stuck, idle), and the one question or the choice you need answered; (2) set `STATUS: STUCK`; (3) commit and push; (4) **stop touching the phone and the HackRF**. Claude's loop reads the file every 10 minutes (overnight too, until 09:00 on 2026-10-26, then as the owner sets it) and answers under "## Current task" with the next move and `STATUS: BENCH`. Never improvise a firmware change that is not in the spec or the current task to get past an obstacle; a Stuck block costs ten minutes, an improvised patch can cost the module.

Keys start no earlier than 2 minutes after this line. Grok: push "HackRF wanted" and I hand it over after the current key.**

- **Recovery, in this order, before declaring STUCK for a silent module:** DIAG `restartmodule` and read `0x34`; a module power-cycle (`diagPowerCycle`) and read `0x34`; the Module firmware page's Restore (the factory image from the verified backup) and read `0x34`. One try each, each result in the Stuck block if it comes to that. A HackRF that does not stop is power-cycled at the PortaPack, never left running. Nothing in recovery keys the radio.
  - Grok Build: while the status is `REVIEW`, wait and re-check this file every few minutes (`git pull --rebase`). Do not start new bench work. **Do not end your turn after pushing a report (owner, 2026-10-28): the background `handoff_watch` only writes `ACTION_REQUIRED` to a log, which nothing reads once your turn has ended, so twice the owner had to prod you. Instead, in the same turn, run a foreground wait: `git pull --rebase` every 60 s until `STATUS: BENCH` comes back with a Current task you have not done (or 90 minutes pass), then carry straight on with it. Only `STATUS: OWNER` ends your turn.**
- **`STATUS: OWNER`**: someone needs the owner (a decision, or anything outside the standing approval). Write the question under "Owner question", push, and stop until the owner answers.
- **Stop and set `STATUS: OWNER`, without guessing,** if anything is unclear, a stop condition triggers, or a step would need something outside the standing approval.
- **A review that ends with "then build X" or "next is X" is the go-ahead for X.** No further handoff follows it: once the small fix the review asked for is committed, start X in the same session (owner, 2026-10-25, after FW21 waited four hours for a go that had already been given). **A step ends with its report in `results.md`, never with a commit of fixes.** When a review lists changes and then a pass list, the changes and the pass list are one step: commit the changes and run the pass list in the same session, then report (owner, 2026-10-25, after the firmware page stopped twice at the commit).

## Standing approval (owner, 2026-10-24, until the owner says otherwise)
**Bench practice (Claude, 2026-10-26 21:35, from the symbol-stream recovery):** after any image that hung, took a wild branch or was restarted mid-command, load the factory image and key it once (3 s stock PTT on a digital channel, status 3 seen) before loading the next bench image. When a bench image stops radiating with no change in its bytes, the factory load is the first recovery, before power cycles and tunes: image reloads, `restartmodule`, a 15 s `dmr009` power-off and analog or digital tunes did not clear the 20:44–21:01 silence; the factory load did.


**Principle (owner, 2026-10-24): the whole bench is inside the Faraday cage. Anything Grok needs to do is allowed, as long as it does not risk damaging the equipment.** The earlier per-item allowances (467.625 MHz only, low power only, HackRF receive only, the R-C tone list) were regulatory precautions and are superseded by this principle. What remains are equipment-protection rules, scope rules the owner set earlier, and the measurement discipline. `STATUS: OWNER` is for a step that could damage hardware, destroy data or configuration irrecoverably, or that lies outside the scope rules; nothing else needs to wait.

**Bench setup:** the phone and the HackRF are next to each other inside the cage and stay there. No per-run confirmation.

**Equipment-protection rules (these are the limits):**
- **HackRF receive front end.** Its input must stay well under its damage level. The phone keyed at close range already drives the IQ into clipping at low power and LNA 24 (R4k/R4o: clip fraction 0.3–0.4). High power at close range is allowed only with a documented margin: an attenuator in line, or distance, or the gains at minimum with the capture's clip fraction near 0; say in the report which. Never key the phone while the HackRF is transmitting next to it.
- **HackRF transmit.** Allowed on any frequency inside the cage, at the lowest gain that gives the needed level. Never from the outdoor antenna. Housekeeping that protects the device and the bench: a dummy burst first (the first transmission after a pause is often silent), never a backgrounded `-R` capture, check nothing else holds the device, power-cycle it if a transfer is stuck.
- **Module firmware.** Only the documented YModem path; never a raw flash command. Every image carries its tag; the factory restore path is proven (ch. 19 §14) and is the recovery for a bad load. Stubs on the key-up path act only on a host-requested transmission and do nothing at module boot (ch. 19 §23).
- **Phone and codec.** ES8311 register **reads** (count 0 on the debug node) are allowed. ES8311 **writes** only for a register whose effect and reset path are understood and written down first, never anything that drives the speaker or amplifier outside its ratings. No `adb uninstall` (it destroys the app data and the Magisk grant). A phone reboot is a last resort for recovery, reported when done, with adb confirmed back afterwards.
- **Heat (measured 2026-10-24, FW19; `results.md`, Heat).** The rises are small: battery (`dumpsys battery`, tenths of °C) +0.2 °C after a 30 s low-power key, +0.5 after a 45 s low-power stream, +0.6 after a 30 s high-power key; the AP and modem-PA zones (`mtktsAP`, `mtktsbtsmdpa`, `mtktsbtsnrpa`) +1 °C after 30 s low power and +3.3 °C after 45 s low power or 30 s high power, down again within a few minutes. The battery reading drifts by half a degree on its own, so it is not the cool-down gate. One long or high-power key at a time. The 120 s high-power stream (2026-10-25 00:01, `results.md`) raised `mtktsAP` by 11.3 °C (47.9 to 59.2) and the modem-PA zones by about 11 °C; the battery rose 1.2 °C during the key and 1.7 °C by three minutes later. It did not reach 60 °C and did not rise more than 2 °C inside any 10 s. Returning to within 1 °C of the pre-key reading took about 17 minutes, so a key of that size waits for the return rather than a 10 minute cap. Shorter keys still wait until `mtktsAP` is within 1 °C of its pre-key value, or 10 minutes, whichever comes first. Stop during a key: battery above 45 °C, or `mtktsAP` or `mtktsbtsmdpa` above 60 °C, or more than 2 °C up inside 10 s. The 1 °C in 10 s stop is retired. The CPU zone (swings several degrees while the radio is idle), `mtktspa` samples of exactly 25.0 °C, `tzimgs*` and `mtktscharger` (−127 °C) are not inputs. High-power phone TX beside the HackRF: LNA 0 / VGA 0 (clip fraction 0, peak 13–14 of 127), as measured.

**Scope rules (the owner's, not safety; lifting them is the owner's call):** no band-limit or power patches (ch. 19 scope). **The time-out ceiling of 120 s is approved by the owner (2026-10-24), not off.** FW19 maps a `0x3B` byte of 0 or above 120 to 120 (the stock handler has no clamp, and 0 turns the module's timer off); SignalDeck sends 120 and never 0; its own watchdog stays the real limit, raised per mode only to what the mode needs, never above 120 s, and long keys at any power run under the heat rule above (measured through 120 s high power, 2026-10-25).

**Measurement discipline (unchanged):** tune first; check the banner and the `0x34` tag after every load; the ±5 kHz deviation rule on what we transmit, and a bin at or over it is reported as a stop (a test that needs more, such as ±4.5 kHz POCSAG with margin, says so in its task line first); boot capture on every image; `git pull --rebase` before every load.

**Defaults for comparability:** 467.625 MHz and low power stay the default bench settings so numbers compare across images; other frequencies and powers are allowed when a test needs them (VHF needs `band=1`), and the report says what was used.

**End state after a run:**
- SignalDeck owns the radio;
- no `DMRDEBUG.bin`;
- the test image may stay loaded. Owner, 2026-10-24: do not flash the factory image after every test. Load the next test image over the one already in the module. Flash the factory image (`0x34` `DMR003.UV4T.V022`) when there is a reason: a stop, a bad banner or tag, an unknown image, boot radiation, or leaving a known-good radio at the end of a session.

The radio may stay on 467.625 MHz, low power: there is no need to return to the NOAA channel.

**Owner rule (08:30): the hotspot is not a trusted receiver on its own. No result may count as a failure on the hotspot's word alone.** It has failed before. For every DMR or M17 check, a second receiver must agree:

- **DMR:** the Atom XL on the same channel (DMR, the bench colour code and slot) receiving the call with the caller ID, plus HackRF bursts at the right slot timing, or the HackRF capture decoded offline.  
- If the hotspot disagrees with the HackRF or the Atom XL, the hotspot is the suspect: write it as **hotspot fault, not a radio result**, and carry on. A 'pass' on the hotspot alone is fine; a 'fail' needs confirmation.  
- Lease the HackRF and the Atom XL in `docs/atomxl-status.md` as usual. This applies to the 08:25 DMR check too: confirm the channel 49 or TS2 key with the Atom XL before changing anything on the hotspot.

The owner says the earlier safety stop was over-cautious, and lets you try what you need to get the manufacturer's official V076 onto the Atom XL's module, **real load included (no further go needed)**, and to document it for future flashes.
   - **Goal:** the module answers `0x34` with `DMR006.U4A.V076`, and the stock app and SignalDeck both work afterwards.
   - **Phone:** Unihertz Atom XL, adb `ATOMXL0000000737`. Powering the radio on takes the only USB port, so use Wi-Fi ADB `192.168.1.106:5555` (persisted). Root via Magisk. Take a lease from the Atom XL agent in `docs/atomxl-status.md` first, and hand back when done.
   - **Image:** `D:/Documents/code/personal/AndroidRadio_Firmware_Project/firmware/unihertz-atom-xl/DMR006.U4A.V076/DMR006.U4A.V076.bin`, sha256 `4f5f287bef875fad4a5c8ca9a94c9e841a21f5b507f2cc2a0645d5947e4d40fe`. It equals the phone's own `/vendor/data/dmr/DMR006.U4A.V076.bin`. The module now runs `DMR006.U4A.V067`, and **no V067 image exists anywhere**, so this is one-way.
   - **The vendor's update sequence** (`docs/atomxl-v076-firmware-analysis.md` §9, read from the HAL `extUpdateDmr`):
     - Start: `extStartMcu`, then the bridge `00 10 00` (module off) and 4 s, `handle_ptt` 0 and 2 s, update mode `00 08 01` and 2 s, `00 10 01` (module on) and 2 s.
     - Then `'1'`, wait for six `'C'` (up to 60 reads), then YModem-1K with CRC-16 (block 0 = file name + size).
     - **Every byte goes inside a route-00 command-`0x09` envelope** (`7E 96 69 00 09 01 0001 <byte> ck 81`). Data goes as ≤ 256-byte `0x09` frames with 100 ms after each full chunk, and the bootloader's replies come back one byte per 12-byte envelope.
     - Clean-up: `00 10 00`, `handle_ptt` 1, `00 08 00`, `extStopMcu`.
   - **Why the vendor route fails on this phone:** both attempts got 0 `'C'` (cCount 0), and nothing was written. In test D1, after `00 10 00` / `00 10 01` the bridge kept answering but the module stayed silent for 30 s; only an `mcu_power` cycle brought it back. So on bridge `20201016161541` the `00 10 01` module-on step never powers the module, and the module-on has to come from `mcu_power` (`/sys/bus/platform/drivers/mcu/mcu_power`). The other bridge nodes are `handle_ptt`, `usb_path`, `rstmcu` and `cmode`; the bring-up and release recipes are in `PHASE_A_FINDINGS.md` §1, §7, §8 and §10 (branch `atomxl/phase-a`). Note that a power cycle can drop `ttyACM0`: re-enumerate and reopen.
   - **Code you can reuse:** SignalDeck `AgoldFrames`/`AgoldEnvelope` (frame build, checksum) and `AgoldUsb` (bring-up, release, the tty wait), plus SignalDeck's Armor YModem sender (the loader). The pulled HAL, stock app and tools are in `D:/Documents/code/personal/phonedmrapp-atomxl/pulled/`.
   - **Suggested order:** a dry run first, up to the six `C` (abort without data), to prove the entry; then the full load. If the module ever stops answering `0x34` after a failed load: retry the bootloader entry (an empty or partial flash usually still enters the bootloader); don't power-cycle in a loop.
   - **Document for future flashes:** `docs/atomxl/firmware-update-howto.md` (the Atom XL agent starts it; you add the working procedure). Also write what a SignalDeck loader needs (the envelope-wrapped YModem, the bridge power and PTT control, timing, recovery) as `docs/atomxl/loader-requirements.md`. Add a results row to `results.md` and a note in the firmware project's Atom XL device page.
   - **Afterwards,** hand the phone back to the Atom XL agent for its V076 tests.
Claude tests alongside when you hand over a lease; write "Armor free for Claude HH:MM–HH:MM" here when there's a natural gap.

**Now (12:10, Claude, review of the re-recording):** the pad is on the air and accepted (`reviews.md`, "POCSAG pad on the air; the last word's dip is the 17-bit padding run"). **The dip at bits 806–822 is inside the last word and is the 17 padding bits after the text** (63 text bits, 3 in the last word, then 17 pad bits of one level, then the check field): a 33 ms run through the 1.6 ms high-pass decays to about zero, which is the "about 200", and the inverse with a τ fitted on a 12-bit run plus a clip-wide mean is not accurate enough over 33 ms, so the check field reads wrong. Real pagers pad the same way. **Next, host first on the two new clips:** (1) fit τ from the run itself (exponential fit to bits 806–822, report it), apply the inverse with that τ and remove the mean measured over the preamble, not the whole clip; both clips must decode payload-exact and stay as the regression clips; (2) end the text at the first NUL (0x00) or EOT (0x04) and never render padding ("ALPHA ONUp"); (3) if the run still falls short of the eye, hold the previous decision inside a run until the restored level crosses the threshold by a quarter of the eye; (4) then 512 baud 3/3 exact on the air and 1200/2400 once more. Part A closes on that; report with STATUS REVIEW; then part B on `grok/web-pager`.

**Now (11:45, Claude, review of the cadence and leak step):** the sync cadence is accepted and the suite is green here (392/0); the leak was my wrong call: keep the exact inverse with the mean removed and no leak on the 512 path (`reviews.md`, "POCSAG sync cadence accepted; the last character is the bit clock"). **The last character is the bit clock:** `follow()` steps a fixed `spb` from the preamble's estimate and re-aligns only at a sync word, so a rate error of 0.15 % puts the sampling point a bit late by the end of a 544-bit batch, which is why the damage sits in the last message word, why it happened once at 1200 too, and why the leak could not help. **Next, host first on `rx_restore_b.wav` and the held clip:** (1) measure: bit errors per word against the sent words before BCH (must climb toward the last word) and the sampling-phase error at the batch's last transition; (2) refine the rate from the syncs, `spb = (sync2 − sync1)/544`, used from the second batch on (idle words after a one-batch page are sync-timed too); (3) track the clock inside the batch: at each transition move `pos` about 1/8 of the way from the zero crossing to the nearest bit boundary (the research `rx/pocsag_pll.py` did this); (4) both clips decode with the full text and stay as regression tests, then 512 baud 3/3 on the air with the pad and the 1200/2400 run once more. **Owner instruction (19:05, via Claude): after the current step's report, before anything else on the phone, make Wi-Fi ADB stick.** The owner's decision on the open question. With the USB cable attached (it stays the fallback transport): as root on the phone, `setprop persist.adb.tcp.port 5555` (the persisted property, which `adb tcpip` alone never sets), then `adb tcpip 5555`, then `adb connect <phone Wi-Fi IP>:5555` and confirm `adb devices` lists both the USB serial and the Wi-Fi address. Then prove it sticks: `adb reboot` once (USB attached, so a failure cannot strand the bench), wait for boot, and `adb connect` again **without** running `tcpip`; it must connect. Record the IP, the property read-back (`getprop persist.adb.tcp.port`) and the connect result in `results.md`. If "Own the radio at boot" is off, SignalDeck will not own the radio after the reboot: take it over again as usual and say so. The USB cable stays attached afterwards; both transports are to be used with `-s`. Nothing of the stream work changes.
1. **Ignored or hung.** On a fresh FW27 boot, send and timestamp every frame and every answer: `0x34`; op 14 read of `0x4B360`–`0x4B38F` (the stub's state words: return address, rate flag, 30 s count, period, slot, switch, UART index, saved returns, rate record); `0x26` down; **`0x34` within one second**; `0x26` up; `0x34`; op 14 of the same words again. If the `0x34` after the down answers, the frame was dropped (then say which path drops it); if nothing answers after the down, the handler hung inside something FW27 changed. Also say whether the tone test's other frames on FW27 (the channel write `0x23`/`0x25`, `0x3B`, the `0x40` tone setup) were acked; the runner's log has them.
2. **Stale RAM or image.** The stub's words are not cleared at boot if the boot code does not zero that region; a flag left set by the 12:xx stream (the stop path never cleared it) would make the FW27 frame hook treat every later frame as the stream's end and drop it. Repeat step 1 after a true cold start: `dmr009` low for ten seconds so the SRAM loses its state, then boot. If the words read zero and `0x26` acks, the bug is the missing boot clear (FW26's op 15 flag got one; give every FW27 word the same) plus the stop path; if `0x26` still fails on a cold start, it is the image.
3. **Which site.** Say what the `0x7C56` hook does (it is the one site outside the `0x40` handler; if it is on the frame receive path or the main loop, it is the first suspect) and whether the `0x26` path or the UART receive path crosses any FW27 site. If steps 1 and 2 do not name it, bisect: FW27 variants with one site restored to FW26 bytes each, `0x34` + `0x26` down/up on each, no RF needed beyond the ack and status 3.
4. **Runner:** `tools/fw-bench/runner/fm.py` must report NO CARRIER when the capture RMS is within 6 dB of the quietest one-second window, instead of "carrier 16 s" at the floor; the tone test then fails with that word. Small, host-only, after 1–3.
Then the fix, `tone` first, `all`, and the queued items (stop path, half rate, quiet run, then (c)). STATUS STUCK with the step 1 and 2 timelines if they do not name the site; REVIEW when FW27 keys and `all` passes.
1. **RAM map, not code diff.** List every RAM address the FW27 stub reads or writes (every `st.*`/`ld.*` target and every address it builds), against `symbols.csv` and the FW21/FW22 engine variables and the stock TX state. Any overlap is the bug. Include the timers that run from the main loop while the stream is off (keepalive, 30 s fallback): a timer that writes a word every tick even when idle corrupts whatever shares it.
2. **Loads into the overwritten body.** Grok listed the branches into `0x5BCA4`–`0x5C098`; list the loads too: every `lrw`/PC-relative load in the image whose literal lies in that range, and every absolute word in the image pointing into it (an engine's table or literal pool placed after the opcode 13 body would be a load, not a branch).
3. If 1 or 2 finds it: fix, rebuild, load, runner `tone` first, then `all`. 1. **In the stream loop, store `0x80` to `WORK_MODE` before each copy, exactly the store op 5 does (same value, same register, same place in the sequence: store, then wait for the flip, then copy the half not pointed at).** Run (b) again at 230400: the per-half 1 kHz amplitude and the sequence misses. If the line is there, the model is right.
2. **Then measure the capture window, because the re-arm costs halves:** op 5 waits for two flips after the store before it copies, so a store per half may lose every other half. Run the stream with the store every 1, 2, 4 and 8 halves, and for each setting report how many of the halves between stores carry the line (Goertzel per half) and the miss count. The largest period that keeps every half with the line is the one to ship; if only a store per half works and it costs the alternate half, say so, that is a real property of the chip and the page gets it (the stream then carries half the audio, which M17 cannot use, and the route's answer changes).
3. `0x3A81C` is the stock tune path's mirror of AT1846S `0x58`; the direct I²C write op 6 does leaves it untouched, so it reads 0 and proves nothing. The live value would need the I²C read at `+0x54664`; not needed, (B) shows the bypass state is right.
4. (A) at 57600 (start acked, no PCM, keepalive and stop unanswered) is consistent with the loop saturating the UART at that rate; ignore it now that 230400 works.
Then (c), the 45 s keepalive run with the chosen store period, (d). Phone is yours. STATUS REVIEW with the per-half tables, or STUCK with them if step 1 shows no line.
(A) **Mode 5, then op 17, no op 16** (UART stays at 57600; only about a third of the halves will arrive, their content is the point): 10 s tone, capture, Goertzel per half. A line here means op 17 is innocent and op 16 is the culprit.
(B) **Mode 5, then op 16 to 230400, then a plain op 5 dump at 230400, no op 17**: a line here means op 16 is innocent and op 17's start path is the culprit.
Then, by the result:
- **If op 16 breaks it:** its write sequence touches more than the divisor. Compare the IER value it restores with the value boot leaves (read IER once at boot time through a diagnostic reply and once after op 16); check that the LCR written back has the boot word length and parity (a wrong LCR still passes the `0x34` round trip only by luck); and check that nothing in the op 16 path calls the stock UART init, which on this image also re-runs the channel apply (`at1846_tune` writes `0x8405`, and the channel write clears mode 5). The register read-back after op 16 (`WORK_MODE`, `FM_BANDWIDTH`, AT1846S `0x58` through the mirror at `0x3a81c`) says whether mode 5 survived the switch.
- **If op 17 breaks it:** the page says "any other command ends the stream and mode 5 is restored before that command runs"; make sure the start command itself, the keepalive, and each pass of the loop do not take that restore path (a loop that exits and re-enters the handler per half would restore mode 5 at every exit). Return the three register words in the first stream frame's spare bytes (after 513) as the proof, compared with their values right after mode 5 action 1.
The main-loop design for the send stays the product design (the page keeps it), but it is not the fix for this symptom. Phone is yours. STATUS REVIEW with (A), (B) and the fix, or STUCK with the two results if neither names the culprit.
1. **Prove it from the capture you already have** (the 11:49:48 run, 344 halves): compute the 1 kHz line amplitude (Goertzel) per half for halves 0..40. The hypothesis predicts the line in the first few halves and a decay to the 3000-rms noise floor within the first second. Report the per-half numbers for halves 0, 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32.
2. **The fix is the one I flagged as the product design on the page at 08:55: move the half send out of the handler into the main loop.** Op 17 action 1 only arms a state (a flag plus the slot and sequence counters) and returns its ack; the poll of `FM_ADDR_SW` and the copy-and-send run from the main loop's periodic hook, the place FW19's time-out timer already runs each wake, one half per pass when the pointer has flipped; action 0 and any other command clear the flag; the keepalive stays action 2. The main loop then keeps servicing the receiver between halves. Budget: one 22.8 ms send per 32 ms half still fits if the hook is reached at least once per half; say how often the loop reaches the timer's hook (the timer counts 100 wakes per second, so the hook runs every 10 ms, which is enough). Space: the loop body moves, it does not grow; the hook needs one redirected call site, the FW19 pattern.
3. Then (b) again: the line in the stream with 0 misses, the phone clip silent (already known), then (c), the 45 s keepalive run, (d).
If step 1 shows the line present through the whole capture, the hypothesis is wrong and the next report says so with the numbers; then the raw single-frame comparison is the move. Two small things for the record: op 5's two halves both carried half id 0 (sequence 0 then 2), so the half id is not reliable as a miss detector; PR 14 now counts misses by the sequence alone. Phone is yours. STATUS REVIEW with the numbers, or STUCK with step 1's table.
1. **The splitting measurement:** with mode 5 held and the same 10 s tone running, send a plain op 5 dump (mode 0, four halves) in the middle of it (the UART at 57600 is fine for this; no op 16, no op 17). If op 5's four halves show the 1 kHz line (rms about 6900 as RC40/R-E had, lag-1 about 0.7), the register state is right and the stream's copy is the bug. If op 5's halves are noise too, mode 5's register state differs from variant 4's and the copy is not the question yet.
2. **If the copy is the bug:** the stream must use op 5's own copy routine, byte for byte, not a re-implementation: same source address for the half (`1` → `0x160006E0`, `2` → `0x160004E0`), same byte-wise loads (RC30: a word read of this 8-bit-wide RAM returns one valid lane per word, which is exactly a "every other sample wrong" picture), same stride and destination. Disassemble your loop's copy next to op 5's and diff them; then call op 5's subroutine from the loop instead. Also confirm the sequence/half-id bytes are written after the samples, not into sample 255's bytes.
3. **If the register state is the bug:** build the diagnostic you proposed: one op that returns the first half's raw bytes plus the words it copied under (`WORK_MODE` at `0x11000100`, `FM_BANDWIDTH` at `0x11000500`, `FM_ADDR_SW` at `0x1100056C`), and compare them with the variant-4 state RC40 recorded; the differences are the fix. Check in particular that mode 5's action 1 writes `WORK_MODE` after the AT1846S write and the band-pass bits, in the order variant 4 does, and that nothing re-sends the audio-path hold (variant 3's `0x8405` restore) while mode 5 is up.
4. **Do not add a wait inside the 32 ms:** the half `FM_ADDR_SW` is not pointing at is complete when the pointer flips, which is why op 5 copies it without waiting; a delay would only hide a wrong address.
Record as a fact: **the phone's speaker is silent while mode 5 is on** (the audio path has no signal in the `0x80` state), so bench (c) compares the stream decode with channel 56's listing of a second transmission of the same file with mode 5 off, and SignalDeck's help will say the stream receiver silences the speaker. Then (b) with the line, (c), the 45 s keepalive run, (d). Phone is yours. STATUS REVIEW with the numbers, or STUCK with the op 5 comparison from step 1 if steps 2/3 do not resolve it.

1. **The RAM fill beside the hold.** The receive mirror fills only with the `WORK_MODE` `0x80` state (RC20); since RE10 op 5 refuses registers while the op 6 hold marker is set. Design the state the stream needs as one op 6 variant (the AT1846S bypass held **and** `0x80` with the FM band-pass bits clear), saved and restored exactly as the hold is today, cleared by any channel write like the hold, with op 5 allowed to read while that variant is in force.
2. **The UART speed switch.** 230400 is reachable (+0.160 %); design the op: the host asks, the module acks at the old rate, switches, and the host reopens at the new rate (SignalDeck's serial JNI takes the rate from the profile, so say what the host does). Safety: the module falls back to 57600 by itself after N seconds with no valid frame from the host, and always at boot; the loader is never entered at 230400; the first command after the switch is a `0x34` so both sides prove the rate.
3. **The stream op.** A push stream, not polled halves: every completed half (256 samples, big-endian as the RAM holds them) goes out as one frame carrying a 16-bit sequence number and the half id, so a missed half is counted; stop on a stop op or on any other command from the host; nothing else may use the UART while it runs (the engine already drops framed commands during a load, say whether that path is reused). Budget: 514 bytes per 32 ms at 230400 is 22 ms on the wire, say what margin is left for the status pushes the module sends on its own.
4. **Bench plan for FW27:** (a) switch round trip and fallback (switch, `0x34` at 230400, idle past N seconds, `0x34` at 57600 again; the loader refused at 230400); (b) a 1 kHz HackRF tone streamed for 10 s: the line's frequency in the stream gives the true sample rate, the sequence numbers give the gap count (expected 0); (c) a 30-frame M17 transmission at −88 dBm decoded **plain** from the stream (no equaliser: the RAM baseband was flat in RC40), payload-exact count, then with the fixed taps for comparison; (d) the regression runner `all`. Pass for the route: (b) with 0 gaps and (c) at or above the audio path's count on the same transmission.
5. **What stays out:** band limits, power, transmit; no change to the hold's behaviour for the audio-path receiver, which remains the default route.

**R4t passed review** (`reviews.md`, "Review of R4t"): ch. 19 §6 M17 transmit acceptance met with our decoder (50/50, then 90/90 × 3 at Golay 0, p99 2792–2895 Hz). Do not key that stream again. **R-C step 4 reviewed** (`reviews.md`, "Review of R-C step 4, RC40"): **the receive mirror exists.** In the variant-4 state (`WORK_MODE` `0x80`, `FM_BANDWIDTH` bits 4 and 6 clear, AT1846S `0x58` = `0xBCFD`) the receive RAM holds a flat baseband: 300 Hz −2.3 dB, 100 Hz +0.3 dB, 50 Hz −0.3 dB against 1 kHz. Each bypass alone is about −20 dB at 100 Hz, so two filters sit in series (AT1846S, then the C7000's band-pass and de-emphasis). The RC40 end-state sentence "the receive design stays the audio-path equaliser" is withdrawn: R-C is **open and positive**; R-B closes (the ES8311 is at most a second-order contributor). RD10 is loaded over RC40.

**R-D reviewed** (`reviews.md`, "Review of R-D, RD10"): **one AT1846S write flattens the phone's own receive audio.** Op 6 variant 3 (`0x58` = `0xBCFD`) puts the phone path at 50 Hz −0.6, 100 Hz +1.2, 300 Hz −1.6, 2 kHz +1.8, 3.5 kHz +1.7, 3.8 kHz +1.7 dB against 1 kHz (plain: −27.6, −24.5, +5.7, −4.0, −12.1, −17.0). Variant 4 is identical, so the C7000 filters are not in the phone's path; the whole cut was the AT1846S voice filter. Any channel write clears it (`at1846_tune` writes `0x8405`). **First receive route is now R-E: that write held while the phone's existing audio path feeds the decoders; the RX RAM stream is the second route** (230400 is reachable at +0.160 %, the same error as the working 57600 link; 460800 is not). The RX RAM M17 captures ran at about 10 % raw symbol errors (frame numbers decode through the Viterbi, LICH words at 2–3.5 errors each, no CRC): suspects, in order, the new in-place reply headers (RD10's 1 kHz capture read 4795 with rms 6648 against RC40's 7707–8499 in the same state and settings), the unmeasured RX sample rate, the C7000 shape. Op 6 as built shares `0x4A3F8` with op 5 and restores a stale `WORK_MODE`. RD10 stays loaded. Do not key it.

**R-E reviewed** (`reviews.md`, "Review of R-E"): **the held phone path is a linear channel and a fixed equaliser decodes it.** On the saved clips the symbol means sit at ±0.97/±3.01 with no cubic term; the 19 % plain symbol errors are a long linear distortion (a ~9 dB dip at 400–600 Hz that the tone table skipped, plus the AC coupling's low-end group delay). A ±20-symbol linear equaliser fitted on one R-E clip decodes the other at 0 symbol errors, LSF CRC 13/13, Golay 0 25/25, and the reverse (`tools/m17-research/heldpath_eq.py`). **Receive design settled: op 6 variant 3 held + a fixed linear equaliser in front of SignalDeck's existing audio path and plain decoder.** R-E's integrity work stands: RX RAM is 8 kHz exactly, halves alternate, in-place headers are innocent, **half 0 of every capture is corrupt** (copied while being written), op 6 fixes verified on RE10, `0x58` writers listed (init, tune, tone setters). RE10 stays loaded. Do not key it.

**R-F reviewed** (`reviews.md`, "Review of R-F"): **passes under the right measure.** The pass I wrote ("Golay 0" = zero raw LICH bit errors) was the wrong one; a receiver counts correct payloads, as SignalDeck's test does. Rescored payload-exact, every clip the decoder locked on is 100 % (75/75, 75/75 at −87 dBm; 73/73, 77/77 at −93; the ±1.9 kHz clips alike), and the taps retrained on random data are the same vector as the loop's (within 0.02). The lock failures were `m17.py`'s acquisition on the unequalised signal (false syncs at 0.86–0.88 from the ±3 lead): **equalising first** (the taps at every sample phase, spacing one symbol) and a 0.95 threshold locks all 18 clips with LSF CRC, 98–100 % payload-exact at −87/−93 dBm and ~95 % at −99 dBm (`tools/m17-research/heldpath_rf_score.py`). The dip is the ES8311 (RAM −0.2 dB at 500 Hz), so the taps are a phone-model table. RE10 stays loaded. Do not key it except the stream-end key.

The 28/28/27 against the 28 bar is most likely the bench, not the receiver: the plain control lost the same three frames, the saved clips lose only the frames past their 4.50 s window (so the live receiver matches the batch decoder frame for frame there), and `hackrf_transfer -n` cancels the USB transfers still in flight when the count is reached (up to ~260 ms at 2 MS/s, six frames). **From now on every bench transmission carries 0.5 s of unmodulated carrier after the EOT.** Open in the live receiver (R-I below): calls longer than 20 s split (`CAP`, no consumption while open, whole-call re-decode every pass), frames at the buffer edge are decoded with the equaliser's look-ahead reading zeros, one sync below 0.6 ends the call, unidentified chains are counted into an open call, and the test polls twice instead of at the thread's cadence and no clip holds an end of transmission. **FW19 reported** (`results.md`). There is no `min(tot, 30)` in the `0x3B` handler. The stub writes 120 when the byte is 0 or above 120. Two low-power streams ran 45.12 s, last chunk op 7, status byte 4 from the module. One 30 s high-power key was measured; the 45 s and 120 s high-power keys were not run because the battery was still 0.5 °C high after 12 minutes. FW19 stays loaded.

**FW19 reviewed** (`reviews.md`, "Review of FW19"): **passes as the ceiling change, and the reading changes the picture.** The stock `0x3B` handler never clamped: it stores the byte, a byte of **0 turns the time-out off**, and a routine at `+0x264` resets the time-out to enable = 1, tot = 120 (the module's own default). The OEM app sends 0 (`sendSetTotCmdToMdl`) and so does SignalDeck's `bootHandshake` (`setTot(0)`), so on stock firmware SignalDeck has no module time-out at all today; the 30 s of 2026-10-16 was the apps' own countdowns (OEM `PERSON_LIMIT_SEND_TIME = 30`, DMRModHooks `TX_LIMIT_S = 30`). FW19 stays loaded as the floor that turns that 0 into 120. **The 120 s ceiling is measured:** the second stream keyed at 22:13:11.068 with no `0x26` afterwards, and the second status byte 4 at 22:15:11 is key-on + 120.0 s, the stored 120 reaching the timer. That same line shows a defect: **op 7's `drop_ptt` drops the carrier but leaves the TX bits of the state byte at `0x38908` set**, so the time-out count at `0x38C64+2..5` kept running 75 s past the carrier and then ran the stock stop path (stray `SEND_STOP`; a second stream inside the window is cut when the first one's 120 s run out; receive during the window unknown). Workaround until FW20: the host sends `0x26` off after the module's status 4 (the first key did, no stray stop).

**Owed from the FW19 task (bench, one session, after the item-2 step in progress):**
1. **High power, 45 s then 120 s** (owner's order), same temperature log, HackRF LNA 0 / VGA 0 with the clip fraction reported: the 45 s stream as run, at high power; cool down per the heat rule; then the ceiling run, a 130 s stream (3250 frames, no end bit) at high power, pass = the module ends the carrier at 120 ± 1 s after key-on, and the report says which status byte it sends then (the OEM app maps 6 to its time-out toast; log 4 or 6). Report the rise per sensor beside the low-power table; the heat rule is rewritten from it.
2. **The timer after op 7.** After the 45 s self-unkey send no `0x26`; log the status bytes for 130 s; 60 s after the drop put a HackRF carrier on 467.625 and read `0x32` and watch for status 1: does the module receive while its TX state is still flagged? Expected: `SEND_STOP` at key-on + 120 s.
3. **One 40 s plain FM key** at low power (a 1 kHz tone through the audio path; the bench extra lifts the 28 s watchdog and the 28.5 s refusal for that run only), pass = carrier 40 s ended by the host. Every key past 30 s so far was a symbol stream; this proves the voice path has no second limit of its own.

**FW20 (firmware, small, after item 2):** `drop_ptt` clears what the `0x26` body-1 path clears (the TX bits of the state byte at `0x38908`, the count at `0x38C64+2..5`), so the timer stops with the carrier. Pass: no status byte within 130 s of the drop, and a second stream keyed 10 s after the first drop runs its full length. **Second part (item 2 review):** delay the drop until the last symbol has left the modulator (count TX RAM half interrupts after the drain, measured); pass adds the end-bit frame and the EOT decoded ("last frame") and 1125 of 1125 on the long stream. **Third criterion (FW19 bench review, 2026-10-25):** within 2 s of the drop the module receives again: `0x32` back at its idle floor and the same weak HackRF carrier moving it as on the idle radio (status 1 if the squelch opens); in the flagged state the receiver is off (measured).

**Host (SignalDeck), done with the M17 voice commit:** `bootHandshake` sends `setTot(120)`, never 0. The symbol-stream sender and the M17 sender send `0x26` off after the module's status 4 until FW20 is loaded. The voice watchdog stays 28 s. The `txPcmOneShot` limit is still 28.5 s; the long SSTV modes stay off until the high-power heat table from item 1 is in. Help that said the radio ends a voice transmission after 30 seconds now says SignalDeck ends it after 28 s and the radio's own limit is 120 s.

**Item 2 is done** (voice check 2026-10-24 23:21). **The owed FW19 bench is done** (`results.md`: high-power 45 s, high-power 120 s ceiling, 40 s FM tone, op-7 timer watch). **FW20 is loaded and measured** (`results.md`): eight halves, then `drop_ptt`, then the stock unkey while the TX bits are still set, then the bits and the count. 1125 of 1125 with the end bit and the EOT, no timer status inside 130 s, a second stream of 70.35 s, and the gain-0 carrier reads the same raw 47 as the idle radio. **The spoken 10 s voice check is done** (`results.md`). **Item 3's opcode space is corrected and not built** (ch. 19, "Item 3"): rational clock, polyphase pulse with a hold mode, tones in eighths of a hertz with continuous phase and an amplitude, queue of 64 pairs. **Next is FW21: build it now**, render first, then the review's keys (a)–(f); the go was given 2026-10-25 04:05 and stands. R-I stays "whenever the bench waits." **Correction reviewed** (`reviews.md`): all four changes in; build FW21. One point to pin in the build: op 3 leaves the M17 stream exactly as FW20 sends it (built-in 5×9 pulse) whether or not an op 9 was ever sent; (e) proves it.

**Item 3 design reviewed** (`reviews.md`, "Review of the item 3 design"): **shape and discipline right; fix four things in ch. 19 before the build, then build FW21.** (1) The symbol clock is rational, not an integer samples per symbol: the TX RAM runs at 8 kHz and M17 already advances 3/5 symbol per sample; POCSAG 512/1200/2400 need 8/125, 3/20, 3/10. Op 8 carries numerator and denominator (uint16) and an exact accumulator. (2) Op 9 is a polyphase table, P phases (1–16) by T taps (odd 1–15), phase = floor(fraction × P); op 8 shaping 0 = hold (NRZ) for the pager path. (3) Tones: frequency as uint16 in eighths of a hertz (FT8's 6.25 Hz = 50), continuous phase across pairs, 0 Hz holds the phase and outputs zero, and an int16 amplitude in TX RAM counts at the head of every op 10/11 body. (4) A tone queue of at least 64 pairs, op 2 reports the pairs remaining, the host paces on it; op 11 arms the end when the queue drains. **FW21 pass:** render first (op 4) for both engines; then at low power under the heat rule: POCSAG 1200 at ±3.5 kHz then ±4.5 kHz (task line says so; 5 kHz stop in force) decoded payload-exact by our decoder and `multimon-ng` if present; MCW 700 Hz 20 wpm decoded; RTTY 45.45/170 one line decoded by SignalDeck's decoder from the clip; one FT8 message decoded by SignalDeck's FT8 decoder from the clip; op 3 then a 90/90 M17 stream; tag, silent boot, a 1125-frame op 7 stream unchanged. Host encoders for the new engines wait for the image; the queued host items (pad out, fuller M17 test, long modes) stay first whenever the bench waits.

**FW21 is loaded and the review's keys have run** (`results.md`, 2026-10-25 09:20). Tag `DMR003.FW21.V022`, boot peak 0.08 dB. Hold, 1 kHz, 700 Hz and an 18.75 Hz offset render match, and op 3 then returns zeros. POCSAG 1200 decoded `HI` at 4061 Hz and 4138 Hz p99. MCW decoded `HI`. RTTY decoded `HI` at mark 1000 Hz. FT8 decoded `CQ N0CALL` at 1000 Hz. M17 after op 3 was 90 of 90, and the 1125-frame op 7 stream was 1125 of 1125 with the end bit and the EOT. Do not rebuild the sine address or the phase load. A tone key must leave one symbol in the ring (`on_key` takes the TX-RAM path only then). Next, whenever the bench waits: remove the two-EOT pad, then the fuller M17 test, then the long modes.

**FW21 reviewed** (`reviews.md`, "Review of FW21"): **passes (a)–(f); item 3 is done.** Follow-ups: (1) **FW22, small:** `on_key` arms the TX RAM path when the ring **or the tone queue** is non-empty (no dummy symbol), plus the **flash read op** from `plan-loader-safety.md` (file offset + length ≤ 240, op 4-style reply); pass = a 700 Hz op 11 key with an empty ring plays the tone, the whole image read back equals the file loaded, the 1125-frame stream unchanged. (2) The pager deviation ceiling on this path is ~4.1 kHz p99 (the modulator compresses; recorded, no action). (3) Host, check: `CQ N0CALL FN20` unpacked as `CQ N0CALL`, so SignalDeck's FT8 CQ may be losing its grid; one test against known vectors. (4) Host, after the queued items: route POCSAG, CW, RTTY and FT8 transmit through ops 8–11 on a bench tag, audio path kept for stock, help updated. **FW22 is loaded and measured** (`results.md`, 2026-10-25 09:59). Tag `DMR003.FW22.V022`, boot peak 0.09 dB. An empty-ring 700 Hz op 11 played, peak 700 Hz, body p99 2060 Hz. Opcode 12 read all 378620 bytes back equal to the file. The 1125-frame op 7 stream was 1125 of 1125 with the end bit and the EOT, p99 2953 Hz. **FW22 reviewed** (`reviews.md`): **passes.** Two asks for later, not blocking: the loader page verifies a load in the background or by sample (a full opcode 12 read is 971 s at the bench UART rate), and FW21/FW22 become reproducible from the repository (commit the generated `fw21_ext.S` under `stubs/`, register the sites in `patches.toml` or have `check.py` read the script's site list, record the reproduced sha256). **The two Phase E reading sessions are written** (`plan-loader-safety.md`, 2026-10-25): update mode is the GPIO knock then `0x31`, and the stock image has no flash read. **Reading sessions reviewed** (`reviews.md`, "Review of the Phase E reading sessions"): accepted; the order changes to **Path B first**, since the knock and the handshake are fully known and SignalDeck already drives the `dmr009` GPIO and the UART, with Path A as the fallback. **Phase E step 1 (bench): SignalDeck's own YModem sender as a DIAG extra**, pass = FW22 over itself (tag + 4 KiB opcode 12 sample), the factory image (tag + a plain key), FW22 again, **one transfer aborted at ~50 % and recovered by the knock and a resend**, silent boots. **Step 2 (host): the page** per `plan-loader-safety.md` with its own pass list in the review, **and per its new section "The page itself" (owner, 2026-10-25): a first-class themed screen, no stock widgets, the pre-flight list on screen, a detailed phased progress bar with live block/byte/rate/time figures and an expandable handshake log, a one-line result, recovery mode on the same page, help with screenshots.** Fold in the FW21/FW22 reproducibility ask before the catalogue lists them. The bootloader checks nothing (no MD5, no signature): the hash checks are the only guard.

**Phase E step 1 passed** (`results.md`, 2026-10-25 13:33, SignalDeck `4fc1989d`). **Reviewed** (`reviews.md`, "Review of Phase E step 1"): **passes all five proofs**; `YModemSenderTest` re-run 7 of 7. The early cancel at block 5 (the intercom page's signal poll writing onto the port mid-transfer) is the finding: during a load nothing else may touch the port, and the page shows "radio busy, loading firmware" to every other screen, Web and Android Auto. Two refinements for step 2, not blocking: give the header its own 60 s wait (the erase took 11.4 s against a 15 s timeout) and show "erasing"; measure the per-block pause at 0 and 50 ms (three loads each, the ACK already gates) and keep the smallest that passes, since the fixed 300 ms is ~110 s of the 248 s load. The five loads used `fwload` only. **Step 2 is the current task:** the loader page per `plan-loader-safety.md` and the review's pass list, including the owner's page section (first-class themed screen, phased progress, live figures, expandable handshake log, recovery on the same page). Fold in the FW21/FW22 reproducibility ask before the catalogue lists those images. The step 1 procedure, already done, was:

1. **SignalDeck, new class `com.macdmr.radio.fw.YModemSender`** (own code from the YModem standard; the decompiled OEM updater was read only to learn the handshake). Inputs: the image file, the profile's `dmr009` `pwd` and `ptt` sysfs nodes, the engine's open serial port with the packet reader paused. Sequence: knock (pwd low, 50 ms, ptt low, 50 ms, pwd high, 1000 ms, ptt high); write `0x31`; wait for `C` (10 s; on silence knock again, three tries, then fail "no bootloader prompt"); block 0 = SOH, seq 0, 128 bytes (file name, NUL, decimal size, zero padding), CRC-16-CCITT; expect `ACK` then `C`; data blocks = STX, seq, ~seq, 1024 bytes (zero-padded), CRC; wait `ACK` 15 s, `NAK` resends, six retries then fail, `CAN` fails; after the last block `EOT`, expect `ACK`; then the empty end block (SOH, seq 0, 128 zeros, CRC), expect `ACK`; then pwd low, 500 ms, pwd high; resume the reader; query `0x34` and return the tag. Every byte received and every state change goes to the DIAG log with a timestamp. Result codes: ok(tag), no-prompt, nak-limit, cancelled, timeout, io-error.
2. **DIAG extra `fwload --es file <path> [--ez abort50]`** in `DiagReceiver`: runs the sender on a file under `/sdcard/Download/DMR/Firmware/`; `abort50` closes the port after half the blocks and returns, so the recovery test can follow with a plain `fwload`. Bench-only; CHANGELOG row; help unchanged.
3. **Bench, in this order, with `fwload_stock.sh` not used:** (a) FW22 over itself: tag `DMR003.FW22.V022`, then opcode 12 of the first 4 KiB equal to the file; (b) the factory image: tag `DMR003.UV4T.V022`, one 10 s low-power analog test tone decodes as a clean carrier; (c) FW22 again, tag; (d) `fwload --ez abort50` with FW22, then `fwload` FW22: tag, and the DIAG log shows the second knock getting `C`; (e) boot capture after (a) and (c) silent. Report the handshake timings from the log (prompt delay, per-block ACK time, total time).
4. **Commit** SignalDeck and the results, then the page (step 2) per `plan-loader-safety.md` and the review's pass list, without waiting for a further handoff. **Bench order from here:** Phase E built against `plan-loader-safety.md`, Path A first. Host, whenever the bench waits: pad out, fuller M17 test, long modes, FT8 grid check, generic engines on the symbol path.

**FW20 reviewed** (`reviews.md`, "Review of FW20"): **passes all three criteria**; the ordering finding (the stock unkey must run while the TX bits are still set, or the receiver stays off until a channel write) is the content of the step and is written down. Carried: the carrier now outlasts the last symbol by ~250 ms (eight halves), accepted; two status 4 bytes per op 7 transmission plus SignalDeck's own unkey, harmless, keep the host unkey. **Host, whenever the bench waits:** remove the two-EOT pad in `m17VoiceRun` (it was "until FW20"), then the fuller M17 encoder test, then the long modes with the cool-down guard (review of the FW19 bench). **The spoken 10 s voice check is done** (`results.md`): 247 of 247, end bit, EOT 0.978, host unkey 13 ms after the first status 4, stock unkey's status 4 at 0.32 s, speech present in the Codec 2 wav. **The opcode space is corrected, not built** (ch. 19, "Item 3", review of `3619aed0`). **Next is FW21.** **Voice check reviewed** (`reviews.md`): passes on every measurable point; the status bytes with the host unkey are exactly FW20's (drop, host unkey 13 ms later, stock unkey at 0.32 s), so the host unkey stays. Intelligibility is the owner's ear: the decoded speech is `C:\Users\Joshua\AppData\Local\Temp\fw1718\sdvoice_out.wav`. Item 3 next, as set; design the op-code space first.

**FW19 bench reviewed** (`reviews.md`, "Review of the FW19 bench"): **complete; every open question answered by a measurement.** Ceiling 120.33 s by the module with status 4 (never 6); the 40 s plain FM key was not ended by the module, so the voice path has no limit of its own; 120 s high power: `mtktsAP` +11.3 °C to 59.2, no stop, back in ~17 min, the rewritten heat paragraph stands; after an op 7 drop without an unkey the module is **deaf** as well as flagged until the timer fires. **FW20 gets a third pass criterion** (receive again within 2 s of the drop, above). **Host, whenever the bench waits, after the fuller M17 encoder test (item 2 review):** the long modes: `txPcmOneShot` per-mode limit up to 118 s (Robot 36, Martin M1/M2, Scottie S1/S2, PD90), voice watchdog stays 28 s, and the cool-down guard from the table: after a transmission longer than 30 s, the next one longer than 30 s waits until the `mtktsAP` zone (sysfs over su; a 17 min timer when unreadable) is within 1 °C of its pre-key value, shown on screen and in the history; a status 4 while PTT is still held gets its own history line (the radio's time-out). It takes the M17 and pager items off the Ubuntu box plan. **Hotspot test queue (owner, 2026-10-27, Claude's session):** (1) SignalDeck decodes the hotspot's M17 transmission on the held path (open: hotspot deviation/polarity/offset); (2) once (1) is reliable, the **stock-firmware receive route** (the DFE receiver, module on the factory image) against the same hotspot transmissions, not a priority; (3) M17 packet mode acceptance; (4) POCSAG and DMR. **Research after the current queue (owner, 2026-10-27): other digital modes (D-Star, System Fusion, P25, NXDN), plan `docs/plan-multimode-digital-research.md`; Grok's first part is a read-only study of the module's DMR vocoder path; not started.** **After the hotspot is up (owner, 2026-10-25): Claude takes the other agent's queued SignalDeck PRs (`docs/handoff-signaldeck-queue-2026-10-25.md`: Soft SQ fixes, name limit, device-report gaps incl. the privacy row, the opt-in firmware-file listing), device-tests and merges them in that order; none of them touches firmware-side code.**
6. **M17 as a channel type (owner, 2026-10-25; host, after the loader page, before the generic engines' host work):** the separate M17 page goes away as a user screen and the intercom page handles M17 the way it handles analog and DMR. Spec: (a) channel editor gains the type M17 with frequency, channel access number, destination (everyone, a callsign, later a reflector) and power; the source callsign is the station's, set once; (b) the intercom's round key keys M17 when the channel is M17 through the existing symbol path, the signal row shows source and destination callsigns and the frame count where DMR shows caller and talkgroup, Last heard, history, announcements, the web intercom, the widget and Android Auto follow through the service's one PTT path; (c) an M17 channel is written to the module as a wide analog FM channel and SignalDeck layers M17 on it: the receive filter hold while the channel is selected (re-sent after channel, VFO, tone writes and PTT), the live receiver on the audio stream, the symbol path on PTT; (d) the firmware gate stays: on stock firmware an M17 channel is receive-only and the editor and help say so; (e) decoder status and calibration move to the Device page beside the firmware line; (f) scan lists with M17 channels and M17 packet data are later items, named here so nobody designs them away. Pass: one M17 channel created in the editor, selected on the intercom, a hotspot or HackRF transmission listed with its callsigns on the intercom, one 10 s key from the round key decoded 100 % payload-exact, the same from the web intercom, R-I's long-call behaviour measured on this path, help and CHANGELOG.
7. **Receive self-calibration (owner, 2026-10-25; after the loader page, alongside item 6):** the fixed per-model equaliser taps become a measurement the phone makes on itself, with no RF. **Reading task first (one session):** which converter drives the phone's ES8311 input in receive, the AT1846S audio output or the HR-C7000's own DAC (the DMR voice path), and whether both enter the codec on the same input; find the register or RAM path that lets our firmware play a known signal out of that converter while the radio is idle and not keyed. **Then one bench session:** a new `0x40` op (next image after the loader needs) plays a 3 s multitone or sweep (50 Hz–3.8 kHz, levels within the ±3 symbol range) out of that converter; SignalDeck records it through its normal audio path; the measured response and group delay are compared with the held-path response measured over the air (R-D/R-E tone tables); pass = the two agree within 1 dB and 0.2 symbols of delay across 100 Hz–3.5 kHz, or a fixed per-model correction is measured and written down. **Host:** a "Calibrate receive" action on the Device page: play, record, fit the 41 taps (port of `tools/m17-research/heldpath_eq.py`), store per phone, show the response before and after; the shipped per-model taps stay the default and the fallback; pass = taps fitted on the Armor decode the 18 R-F clips payload-exact as well as the shipped taps do. **Fallback for stock firmware or when the op is not available:** adaptive training on real receptions using the M17 preamble, sync words and CRC-validated link setups, frozen and stored once converged (the stock-firmware DFE already does the weak form). Help and CHANGELOG with it.
8. **POCSAG page (owner, 2026-10-25; secondary to the M17 items, host, PKT section):** one professional screen for receive and transmit. **Protocol facts the design rests on:** a page is frequency + speed (512/1200/2400 baud, 1200 the default) + RIC (0–2,097,151) + function code (0–3; alphanumeric text is the one for messages) + text; one transmission carries any number of address-plus-message blocks, so several RICs in one key-up is native; POCSAG has no source address, so a sender tag is SignalDeck's own convention at the start of the text (ordinary pagers show it as text, a receiving SignalDeck parses it into a sender and offers Reply). **Screen:** header with frequency and speed (presets, e.g. a DAPNET channel, plus custom) and the station's own RIC(s) and a Listen toggle with status; a received-pages list (time, RIC, function, text; pages to one of the station's RICs highlighted and alerted; Reply where a sender tag was found; monitor shows all); compose at the bottom: a recipients field that opens into a full sheet for many RICs with a small address book (name ↔ RIC, from contacts), message type, text with a counter, a "sign with my RIC" switch, Send, and transfer progress. **Paths:** transmit through the symbol engine (op 8 hold mode + ops 1/7, the FW21 proof; the pager's own 512/1200/2400 advance; the deviation ceiling ~4.1 kHz p99 is the practical level) on a bench or released image only, the audio-path version retired; receive through the held-path decoder (the same 41 taps as M17) on such images, the stock-firmware DFE path as the fallback, with the existing keyword alerts and history. **Pass:** two SignalDecks (or SignalDeck and the hotspot's DAPNET transmitter) page each other: a page to one RIC and a page to three RICs in one transmission decoded payload-exact by the other side and by an independent decoder (`multimon-ng` on the bench PC or the Ubuntu box); a received page with a sender tag shows Reply and the reply arrives; monitor lists pages not addressed to the station without alerting; help topic and CHANGELOG.
9. **Contacts: callsign and pager RIC fields, and one station identity (owner, 2026-10-25; host; prerequisite for items 6 and 8, so it goes before them):** a contact gains a callsign (optional SSID for APRS) and zero or more pager RICs, stored in SignalDeck's own contact store, never in the module's contact table, so stock import and export stay untouched; existing DMR contacts get their callsign filled from the RadioID names already in the app, user-editable. One **station record** (callsign, DMR ID, pager RICs) replaces the callsign in APRS settings and is what M17 transmit, APRS and the POCSAG sender tag use. Uses: M17's destination picker and Last heard match callsigns to contacts; APRS stations match to contacts; the POCSAG page's recipients box accepts names or callsigns and resolves them to RICs, the sender tag in a page is the callsign (the DAPNET convention), and a received page whose tag matches a contact shows the name and offers Reply to that contact's RICs. Backup and restore carry the new fields. **Also (owner, 2026-10-25): a free-text notes field, and location as two separate things: a home location the user enters (Maidenhead grid or coordinates, with a label) that never leaves the phone except where the user explicitly sends it, and a last-heard position the app fills itself from APRS packets, M17 metadata or a DMR GPS report, with its time; the contact shows distance and bearing from the station, the APRS map labels known stations with their contact names, and an automatic fix never overwrites what the user typed.** **Also (owner, 2026-10-25): every callsign, DMR ID or pager RIC the app shows is a tap target** (APRS packets and stations, DMR Last heard and texts, M17 listings, POCSAG pages): a known one opens the contact, an unknown one offers "Add to contacts" or "Add to an existing contact" with the identity, the position and the time already filled in, so a contact is one tap away from the thing that was heard. The same on SignalDeck Web. **Also (owner, 2026-10-25): each contact keeps a history**, newest first, of when it was heard (APRS packet, DMR voice, DMR text, M17, page) with the position when the transmission carried one, and of what was sent to it (a text, a voice call keyed to its ID, an APRS message, an M17 call, a page), shown on the contact's sheet; the last entry is the summary on the card. **Also (owner, 2026-10-25): channels get a history view too**: the card says when the channel was last heard, and tap → History lists everything on it newest first (voice with the DMR caller, texts, tones, Morse repeater identifications and what they said, packet, recordings, the user's own transmissions, alerts) with time and signal; the lines are the intercom's history lines filtered to the channel. **Status 17:12, 2026-10-25: built and device-checked on the `claude/contacts` branch, pull request https://github.com/IIMacGyverII/SignalDeck/pull/4 (owner merges): schema added in place, contact with callsign + two RICs + notes + home created, backup → delete → restore kept every field, an injected APRS packet from the bench callsign filled the last-heard position and a history entry with the home untouched, contact and channel cards and the editor's History section checked by screenshot; the identity tap targets and the web Contacts page are not yet exercised on the phone. **Merged 17:30 as SignalDeck `8e304b0d` (main); item 6 can start.** A DAPNET callsign-to-RIC lookup is a later item, only if their API allows it without an account. Pass: a contact with a callsign, two RICs, notes and a home location survives backup and restore; an APRS packet from a contact's callsign fills its last-heard position without touching the home location; a DMR contact shows its callsign from RadioID; the M17 picker and the POCSAG recipients box both find a contact by name and by callsign; a tap on an unknown APRS callsign and on an unknown pager RIC each creates a contact with the identity filled in, and a tap on a known one opens it; help, CHANGELOG.
10. **Promiscuous DMR receive (owner, 2026-10-25; firmware-side, AFTER the hotspot is up, route-list item 5, because the hotspot is the controllable DMR transmitter that makes the A/B bench automatic):** hear every call on the tuned channel regardless of talkgroup and ID, colour code and (stretch) time slot, toggled from the app over the UART with no reflash, and the received colour code and slot shown by SignalDeck. The spec with every edit site pinned is `docs/promiscuous-rx-patch-spec-2026-10-24.md` (plan `docs/promiscuous-rx-plan-2026-10-24.md`, ideas list `docs/firmware-improvement-ideas-2026-10-24.md`, capture-and-replay harness `tools/dmr-replay/`; all written from the image, nothing bench-tested yet): the talkgroup/ID admit is MCU software (two call sites of the getter at `0x2404`, redirected to a sentinel-aware stub), the colour code is C7000 `LOCAL_CC` `0x11000384` bit 8 (`cc_opt` = match any), the slot is `WORK_MODE` `0x11000100` bit 0 (lowest confidence), and the `0x2B` caller-info reply gains colour code and slot from `RX_TYPE_INFO` `0x11000390` (append-only, length 11). **Split:** Grok builds the image from the spec (one patch at a time: TG bypass, then CC, then the 0x2B extension, then slot) and runs the bench; Claude does the SignalDeck side (the toggle on the channel editor and the intercom, the CC/slot display, the harness scoring) on its branch. **Bench, with the hotspot as the transmitter:** the hotspot sends a known talkgroup/colour-code/slot matrix (TG in the list / not in the list; CC 1 / another CC; slot 1 / slot 2) on 467.700 low power; pass = every combination heard with promiscuous on and the stock behaviour with it off (TG not in the list muted, wrong CC muted), the 1125-frame M17 stream and a plain DMR call unchanged, the toggle survives a channel write, nothing keys at boot. Order: after FW25 and item 6, and not before the hotspot works for a plain DMR call. **Owner, 2026-10-25 22:55: build it before the hotspot arrives, test it with the hotspot.** Spec approved with five amendments at the top of `docs/promiscuous-rx-patch-spec-2026-10-24.md` (toggle = a new op on `0x40`, measure the free stub space first, FW26 on the FW25 lineage, the `0x2B` parser takes 9 or 11 bytes, the A/B rows wait for the hotspot). Split: **Grok** builds FW26 (the flag op, Patch A at the two gate sites, Patch B behind the precheck, Patch D, `patches.toml`, tag, the stub-space measurement and the must-confirm items that can be read statically) after item 7's host part, with no bench key beyond a load, a `0x34` read and the flag written and read back; **Claude** builds the SignalDeck side on `claude/promiscuous` (toggle beside MON on digital channels, parser, display, help, changelog) with a host test on a synthetic 11-byte reply. The A/B pass runs when the hotspot is in the cage. **Claude's side is up (SignalDeck PR 11, `claude/promiscuous`, host-green, 2026-10-25 23:25): op 15 body `[15, v]` with a one-byte echo reply, sent with MON on a digital channel at FW26+; the `0x2B` parser takes 9 or 11 bytes. Grok: build FW26 against that op number and body; the PR merges once FW26 and the hotspot pass.**

Rules: the standing approval above (owner, 2026-10-24: anything in the cage that cannot damage the equipment); T4 closed; `0x3D` untouched; R4l0 never reloaded.

## Goal and route list (owner, 2026-10-23: "discover every possible way we can get M17 to work")

Work the routes in this order. Each step ends in a report (`STATUS: REVIEW`). The next step is the next open item on this list. A route is closed only by a measurement or a firm reading, written up in `results.md`.

**Transmit (the hard part: the FM audio path cuts below ~250 Hz, phone-side analog, ch. 19 §19)**
- **T1: TX RAM through the C7000 FM modulator.** R3a3 through R3a7 put a square on the air through the DMR modulator. Silence leaves the square and drops the codec tone. Through A = 1024 the flat-top median tracked about 0.41 Hz per count. At A = 4096 the 40 Hz low-pass rms was 2266 Hz (0.55 Hz per count), the median was 1462 Hz, and the steady p99 was 3700 Hz. Another doubling of the square is predicted past the ±5 kHz stop, so A = 8192 is not an image. R3a8 put a 1 kHz sine at peak count 2048 on the air: the 1 kHz line was 923 Hz, the 15.625 Hz line was 3 Hz, the 3 kHz line was 27 Hz, and the steady p99 was 1217 Hz. R3a9 put the same sine at peak count 4096 on the air: the 1 kHz line was 1923 Hz, the 15.625 Hz line was 3 Hz, the 3 kHz line was 26 Hz, and the steady p99 was 2186 Hz. R3a10 (tag `R3b0`; the version field is four characters) put the same sine at peak count 5120 on the air: the 1 kHz line was 2261 Hz, the 15.625 Hz line was 3 Hz, the 3 kHz line was 14 Hz, and the steady p99 was 2674 Hz. The tone is 6% under ±2.4 kHz and the steady p99 is 11% over it. The sine scale compressed (1.18× for a 1.25× peak). R4a (tag `R4a0`) put a fixed RRC α = 0.5 pattern on the same path, every sample at or below count 5120. Steady p99 2774 Hz. The stored 10-sample shape is inverted on the air, −0.54 Hz per count, median r² 0.96 on 10 ms stretches. The 1 kHz line is 17 Hz. Four held symbol levels are absent. The long-term line is 796.875 Hz because the last two samples repeat the first two. R4b (tag `R4b0`) put one link-setup frame in that loop: 108 symbols of `0x77`, the 192-symbol LSF, sign flipped, peak count 6451. Steady p99 3148 Hz. The plain decoder accepted 43 of 43 loops, sync 0.999, aimed-waveform r² 0.81 and gain 0.79. A stream frame does not fit behind it in 512 samples. The UART command `0x3D` still needs a SignalDeck sender, which is outside the standing approval, so it waits. The FM refill interrupt was read on 2026-10-24. It is bit 15 of `SYS_INTERP_LIST`. The normal path blocks it. The task at `+0x18A70` clears it and does not copy samples. R4c (tag `R4c0`) was that poll. The `0x26` ack was held 358 ms and `SEND_START` came 7 ms after the ack. The carrier was unmodulated: steady p99 371 Hz, burst p99 346 Hz, 64 ms correlation 0.021, aimed-waveform r² 0. The poll walked the table before the modulator started, and the air looped the zero tail. The peak was not raised. R4d (tag `R4d0`) returned from `on_key` in 5 ms. The hooks are the per-wake sites `+0x18B5C` and `+0x18B68`. The task entry `+0x18A70` is unchanged. `SEND_START` followed the ack by 5 ms. The 304 ms table played once: a decode of 0.04–0.40 s accepted the link setup and frames 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, and `0x8005`, Golay error 0. Steady p99 408 Hz is the zero tail. Burst p99 2758 Hz. The key-up click is not a stop. The peak was not raised. The table already fills the file. R4e (tag `R4e0`) wrapped that cursor. Silent boot. One low-power key on 467.625. The `0x26` ack returned in 4 ms and `SEND_START` followed by 6 ms. Steady p99 3075 Hz, so the carrier stayed modulated. The 240 ms correlation is 0.993. A decode from 0.04 s accepted the link setup and 27 stream frames, Golay error 0, and the window from 1.0 s accepted 53, Golay error 0. Frame numbers are only 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, and `0x8005`. Click p99 22987 Hz is not a stop. The peak was not raised. The end-of-transmission frame repeats every loop. R4f (tag `R4f0`) dropped that frame. Silent boot. One low-power key on 467.625. Frame numbers are only 0, 1, 2, 3, and 4. Click p99 22905 Hz is not a stop. The peak was not raised. There is no end-of-transmission frame. R4h (tag `R4h0`) adds frame 5 with the end bit clear. A sync search finds one frame `0x8005` at 3.685 s, score 0.997, Golay error 0, payload bytes 0 through 15, after frame 5. The fixed last-0.8 s window locked a sync at score 0.911 and missed it. Click p99 22781 Hz and the drop click p99 23392 Hz are not a stop. The peak was not raised. The test image stayed loaded until the owner asked for the factory image. That load is `0x34` `DMR003.UV4T.V022` at 09:45:33.593, VFO 467.625 at 09:45:35.253. R4k (tag `R4k0`) posts status byte 4 from `drop_ptt` through file `+0x7E08`. Silent boot. R4l (tag `R4l0`) pointed command `0x40` at a thunk in the data table at `+0x35F64`. YModem `onSuccess` at 11:21:50.172, then no `0x34`. Factory restored: `0x34` `DMR003.UV4T.V022` at 11:27:03.083.
