From 60 ms through 220 ms the p99 is 2817–3086 Hz, then about 90 Hz. The link-setup CRC at 70 ms passes, sync 0.998, and frames 0, 1, and 2 with the end bit set follow, Golay error 0. The peak was not raised. R4r0 stays loaded. VFO 467.625 at 12:51:21.985. R4s (tag `R4s0`) streams symbols through command `0x40` and runs the RRC in the wake: one key, p99 3660–4109 Hz from 60 ms through 2000 ms, no frame accepted, model fit r = −0.34, and the 90-frame run was not started. R4t (tag `R4t0`) sign-extends the tap load: a no-key render matched 106 of 106 samples, one 50-frame key accepted 50 frames at Golay 0, and three 90-frame keys each accepted 90 frames at Golay 0, with the steady slices from 80 ms between 2792 Hz and 2895 Hz.
- **T2: the C7000 DMR modem as a raw 4FSK source.**
  - `WORK_MODE` `0x22` is the built-in BER test. Read 2026-10-24: byte 3 of `0x34808` is 1 only when flag `0x324D7` is 1. Command `0x3F` sets that flag for the Hongrui pattern and for O.153 (strings at file `+0x38444` and `+0x38454`); the OEM BER screen sends O.153, and both values store the flag as 1. Byte 4 stays 0. The flag==2 arm is in the image and unused by the writers that name the flag. A normal channel leaves both bytes 0, and the first setup stores `0x6A` or `0x6E`. The helpers at `+0x18218`, `+0x186BC`, `+0x28904` and `+0x19438` write modem control words and status bytes. They fill no symbol buffer. No `0x22` image was built.
  - `RF_MODE` bit 7 and the filter controls, read 2026-10-24. The only full-word immediate store is the FM key-up at `+0x3EDDA`: `movi r3, 135; lsli r3, 5` = `0x10E0` (bits 12, 7, 6, and 5 set). Bits 6:5 are two-point. Bit 7 set is Document 2's sign inversion of the same ±1944/±648 Hz map, so +3 maps to −1944 Hz. ±2400 Hz is not on that map. Bit 12 is set and unnamed. `FM_BANDWIDTH` and `FM_DEV_COEF` are never stored, so they stay at reset. No RRC coefficient is written. The α = 0.5 that was on the air was the R4a host fill. The `0x22` path still writes neither `RF_MODE` nor `FM_BANDWIDTH`. No image.
  - Symbol buffer, read 2026-10-24. `+0x2A18` is 23 constant-length copies into modem RAM. The lengths are 2, 4, 9, 24, 27, and 36. The three 27-byte copies land at `0x16000030` and write `0x4D49E + n*27` once. Nothing fills a 256-sample half, and nothing holds PTT while a buffer drains. The only store of PTT = 1 is the FM key-up. No host symbol FIFO. No image.
- **T3: the AT1846S itself.** Read 2026-10-24. The asked registers are FM setup scalars. Programming Guide 1.4 register 30h bit 10 (`direct_reg`, "1=enable direct_reg", no further definition) is clear in every immediate, and no read-modify-write sets it. Guide section 5 `3aH[14:12]` is 000 (Tx nothing) or 100 (microphone) in every stored value, including both bandwidth-profile rows. The OpenRTX web table puts the voice source on `0x3C[15:14]`; the stores are `0x0407` and the 25 kHz profile's `0x1930`, both with bits 15:14 = 00 (microphone). `0x35` and `0x36` are never written. `0x41`, `0x42`, `0x43` and `0x44` are single gain, threshold, or deviation words. No host-symbol path. No image.
- **T4: a hybrid.** Only if T1–T3 all fail: what plain-M17 decode rate a standard receiver gets from the UVm6 path with the best pre-shaping (measured, not assumed). This is the fallback number.

**Receive (works today with the calibrated DFE; these aim at plain or simpler decoding)**
- **R-A: C7000 DMR demodulator symbols.** Read 2026-10-24. The only multi-byte read that starts at `0x160004E0` is the BER scorer's 36-byte snapshot (`+0x27E50`, called from `+0xAD64`). Thirty-three bytes are compared on the MCU. The UART output is a 6-byte score on command `0x21` subcommand 34, and the OEM dispatcher does not register `0x21`. The 27-byte records stay unnamed at `0x4D3AD`. No host symbol FIFO and no discriminator-sample buffer. No image.
- **R-B: the ES8311 ADC high-pass** (receive side, phone kernel). Reading registers with count 0 is a read, but it is still a write to a debug node, so ask the owner first (`STATUS: OWNER`). **Closed 2026-10-24 (RC40): the receive cut is upstream of the ES8311.**
- **R-C: the C7000 FM receive RAM** (guide reading 2026-10-24, `reviews.md`). The user guide (now `radio_firmware/HR_C7000_user_guide.pdf`, §7.2.2.3.2 p. 271, §11.4.2 p. 340) says that in FM mode the FM demodulator output is written to RX RAM `0x1600_04E0`–`0x1600_08DF` by default: two 256-sample halves, big-endian, `FM_ADDR_SW` selects the half, interrupt `SYS_INTERP_LIST` bit 14. The receive mirror of T1. Unknown: whether this radio's analog receive goes through the C7000 at all. Step 1 is reading only (`WORK_MODE` on an analog channel in receive, mask bit 14, readers of `0x160004E0`); step 2 is one boot-gated image that dumps a RX RAM half over the UART on a host command, receive-only bench with a HackRF tone and the sweep. No transmission. Details in `reviews.md`. Step 2, tag `RC20`: op 5 with `WORK_MODE` `0x80` returned 512 bytes, the phone did not radiate, and a 1 kHz HackRF tone is the peak of the 128 non-zero slots from TX VGA 6 up; 100 Hz is not, and the sweep folds above 2 kHz. Step 3, tag `RC30`: a byte copy returns 256 int16 per half, 1 kHz and 300 Hz are present, and 100 Hz stays more than 6 dB below 1 kHz with the band-pass cleared and with `0x58` = `0xBCFD`. Step 4, tag `RC40`: over 128 ms the 100 Hz line is +0.3 dB against 1 kHz only when both bypasses are on together. R-C and R-B close on that measurement. **Status 2026-10-24: open, positive (RC40): flat baseband in RX RAM with the variant-4 register state; streaming design pending (R-D).** **Parked 2026-10-26 (02:15 review): the stream needs a UART switch to 230400 (57600 carries about 36 % of the halves) and the `0x80` state applied beside the op 6 hold (since RE10 op 5 refuses registers while the hold marker is set, so the 01:41 mode-0 dump read an idle RAM, rms 6–14, which is not evidence against RC40); opcode 14 does not reach `0x160004E0`. Reopen only for a phone whose audio path cannot be made flat.** **Reopened by the owner 2026-10-26 08:20 ("let's pursue this"): design step in `plan-rx-ram-stream.md`, then FW27 (the `0x80` state beside the hold, a 230400 switch with fallback, a push stream with sequence numbers) and the SignalDeck stream receiver; see Current task.** R-D, tag `RD10`: the phone path at 100 Hz rises from −24.5 dB to +1.2 dB with `0x58` = `0xBCFD` alone and a VFO write puts it back, RX RAM at 3.8 kHz is +2.2 dB, eight plain M17 captures locked sync with no CRC and no Golay 0 frame, and 230400 is +0.160% on the 48 MHz UART clock. **After R-D (RD10, review): the phone's own audio path is flat with op 6 variant 3 alone, so the first receive route is R-E (the bypass held, the existing audio path); the RAM stream stays the second route; its plain decode is under R-E step 2.** R-E (RD10 and RE10): that held path locks sync and frame numbers 0 and 1 and does not plain-decode, the RAM capture is 8 kHz with errors spread through the frame, and a variant-3 restore alone returns the plain 100 Hz level. R-F: those taps on new callsigns and random payloads reach 57/75 Golay 0 at ±2.4 kHz and VGA 20, and the 500 Hz dip is in the phone audio while the receive RAM stays flat. RG10: the render reply is off the stack, the first receive half matches the others, and one 90-frame key stayed at 2794–2913 Hz with 90 payload-exact frames. **After R-E (review): the held phone path is linear and a fixed ±20-symbol equaliser decodes it at 0 symbol errors; receive design settled on the phone path (R-F validates); the RAM stream stays the second route; RAM half 0 of each capture is corrupt (fix in R-F step 4).** **After R-F (review): receive design final on the held phone path (fixed per-model taps, equalise-first acquisition; 98–100 % payload-exact at −87/−93 dBm, ~95 % at −99 dBm on independent data); the 500 Hz dip is the ES8311, the RAM is flat; RAM = route 2, half-0 fix with the stream-end image.** **After R-H (review): the live receiver follows one transmission on the phone (SignalDeck `943e4ec2`, one listing each, right callsigns); the 27–28 of 30 is most likely `hackrf_transfer -n` cutting the tail (pad bench transmissions with 0.5 s of carrier); calls over 20 s, buffer-edge frames and one lost sync are R-I.**

**Vocoder and protocol (SignalDeck side, later):** Codec 2 3200 already encodes (DIAG `m17 --es enc`). Full M17 voice TX needs the frames (LSF, stream frames, CRC, Golay, convolutional code, puncturing, interleaving and randomising), written from the M17 spec. libm17 is GPL-2.0-only: never port it.

**M17 SMS, packet mode (owner, 2026-10-26 21:35): after M17 voice and the hotspot session, before anything else on M17.** A packet transmission is the link setup (type field = packet), then up to 33 packet frames of 25 payload bytes with a counter/last-frame field, the packet puncturing and sync word, and a CRC-16 over the payload; the payload's first byte is the protocol (0x05 = SMS, then the text, null-terminated). SignalDeck side: a packet frame builder beside the stream one, sent through the symbol engine; the packet sync and decoder on the audio path with the taps for receive; the M17 page gets a text box and a list. Firmware side: none expected. Independent check: SMS from the hotspot's reflector to us and ours seen on the hotspot (the WPSD-M17 fork passes packet mode since October 2026; its hotspot hat needs the packet-capable modem firmware). Exact field widths and the counter semantics to be read from the current spec before coding.

In phonedmrapp both keep committing only their own paths. **The phone is Grok's while a step runs.** Claude uses it in the window after Grok's report lands and before the handoff is updated (Grok waits for the handoff then, so nothing collides): when a device check of Claude's is pending, Claude runs it in that window and commits the handoff update after it, keeping the window short so Grok stays busy (owner, 2026-10-25). Outside that window a device check Claude needs is a short pass item in the handoff for Grok to run. Claude keeps reviewing Grok's reports between its own tasks; the review cadence does not change.
